6516               	;
6517               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
6518               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
6519               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
6520               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
6521               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
6522               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
6523               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
6524               	; 	'
6525               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
6526               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
6527               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
6528               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
6529               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
6530               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
6531               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
6532               	; 	hacc = hacc + hsp4

6821 CDF6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
6822 CDF8 0450  20 	b *r0
6823               	;
6824               	; 	' ======================================================================
6825               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6826               	; 	' ======================================================================
6827               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6828               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6829               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6830               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6831               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6832               	; 	'
6833               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
6834               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
6835               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
6836               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
6837               	; 	' broken.
6838               	; 	'
6839               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
6840               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
6841               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
6842               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
6843               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
6844               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
6845               	; 	'
6846               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
6847               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
6848               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
6849               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
6850               	; coll_obst:
6851               cvb_COLL_OBST
6852               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the

7367               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
7368               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
7369               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
7370               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
7371               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.

7394               cv548
7395               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
7396               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7397               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7398               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7399               	; 	'
7400               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7401               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7402               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7403               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7404               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7405               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7406               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
7407               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

8102               	;
8103               	; 	' ======================================================================
8104               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8105               	; 	' ======================================================================
8106               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8107               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8108               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8109               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8110               	; 	'
8111               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8112               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8113               	; 	'
8114               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8115               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8116               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8117               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8118               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8119               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8120               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8121               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8122               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8123               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8124               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8125               	; esc_tick:
8126               cvb_ESC_TICK
8127               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8128               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8129               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8130               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8131               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8132 D8EA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8EC 2149     
8133 D8EE 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     D8F0 0100     
8134 D8F2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8135 D8F4 1402  14 	jhe cv612
8136 D8F6 0460  28 	b @cv611
     D8F8 D90E     
8137               cv612
8138               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8139 D8FA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8FC 2149     
8140 D8FE 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     D900 0700     
8141 D902 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8142 D904 1A02  14 	jl cv614
8143 D906 0460  28 	b @cv613
     D908 D90E     
8144               cv614
8145 D90A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8146 D90C 0450  20 	b *r0
8147               cv613
8148               	; 	END IF
8149               cv611
8150               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8151               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8152               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8153               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8154               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8155               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8156               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8157               	; 	'
8158               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8159               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8160               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8161               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8162               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8163               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8164               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8165               	; 	escp = escp + 1
8166 D90E D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     D910 20AF

     D914 0100

     D918 20AF

     D924 D92C

8381 DAD8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

8383               	;
8384               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8385               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8386               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8387               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8388               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8389               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8390               	; 	'
8391               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8392               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8393               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8394               	; 	'
8395               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8396               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8397               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8398               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8399               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8400               	; 	'
8401               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8402               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8403               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8404               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8405               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8406               	; 	'
8407               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8408               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8409               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.

8628               	;
8629               	; 	' THE ELEVATOR MARKER TRACKS THE CAR, which is the whole point of it. It
8630               	; 	' used to be a static bar on all three shopping floors -- that says where
8631               	; 	' the shaft is, which never changes and which the player already knows,
8632               	; 	' and says nothing about the one fact that matters: whether the car is
8633               	; 	' where you are. Erase, move, redraw, exactly like an actor's dot.
8634               	; 	' ONE ROW NOW, AND WIDER FOR IT. The band is four pixel rows and each
8635               	; 	' one carries a single colour, so the car's grey row is the same row the
8636               	; 	' escalator heads use; spending a second row on it would put half the
8637               	; 	' car on Kelly's BLACK row, where it would read as the Kop. Five pixels
8638               	; 	' across at a fixed centre column tells it from a three-pixel actor dot
8639               	; 	' without needing the height.

     E61A AA16     
9793               	;
9794               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
9795               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
9796               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
9797               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
9798               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
9799               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
9800               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
9801               	; 	'
9802               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
9803               	; 	'
9804               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
9805               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
9806               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
9807               	; calc_bonus:
9808               cvb_CALC_BONUS
9809               	; 	#bonus = 0
9810 E61C 04E0  34 	clr @cvb__BONUS
     E61E 20D4

9812 E620 D020  34 	movb @cvb_TSEC,r0
     E622 20BD

     E626 0100

     E62E E664     
9817               cv734

     E632 0100     
9820 E634 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_BI
     E636 2146     
9821               cv735
9822               	; 			#bonus = #bonus + bmul

     E63A 2083

9825 E63E C040  18 	mov r0,r1
9826 E640 C020  34 	mov @cvb__BONUS,r0
     E642 20D4

     E648 20D4

10043               	;
10044               	; 	' ======================================================================
10045               	; 	' SOUND
10046               	; 	' ======================================================================
10047               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10048               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10049               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10050               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10051               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
10052               	; sfx_tick:

10400               	;
10401               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10402               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10403               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10404               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10405               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10406               	; 	'
10407               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10408               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10409               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10410               	; 	'
10411               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10412               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10413               	; 	' letter after it.
10414               	;
10415               	; font_bits:
10416               cvb_FONT_BITS
10417               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
10418 EB3A 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     EB3C 0000     
     EB3E 0000     
     EB40 0000

10535               	;
10536               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
10537               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
10538               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
10539               	; 	#if TI994A
10540               	; 	BANK 1
10541      12EC     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

10546               	bank 3
10547               	; 	#endif
10548               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10549               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10550               	; 	'
10551               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10552               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10553               	; 	' being small sprites.
10554               	; 	'
10555               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10556               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10557               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10558               	; 	'
10559               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10560               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10561               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10562               	;
10563               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly RIGHT then LEFT: hat, face, tunic, leg1, leg2, duck-hat, duck-face. Patterns 0..52
10564               cvb_SPR_KELLY

11214               	;
11215               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11216               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11217               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11218               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11219               	;
11220               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11221               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11222               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11223               	; 	' unreadable.
11224               	;
11225               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11226               cvb_SCAN_COL3

11295               	; 	'   156 SCANBK
11296               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11297               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11298               	; 	'
11299               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11300               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11301               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11302               	; 	'
11303               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11304               	; 	' blit is
11305               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11306               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11307               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11308               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11309               	; 	'
11310               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11311               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11312               	; 	'
11313               	; 	' template ids:
11314               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11315               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11316               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11317               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11318               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11319               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11320               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11321               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11322               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11323               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11324               	;
11325               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11326               cvb_STOR_TPL
11327               	; 	DATA BYTE 148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148
